Equations for the finiteness

Lemma 36

(soundness)
.

For each
i\in[1,21]
,
w_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\sim}}_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}v_{i}
.

Proof Sketch.

We prove
w_{i}[a]\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}}v_{i}[a]
, where
a
is any variable.
This equation can be translated to the validity of a first-order sentence via the standard translation
[
30
]
.
Here, we add the formula
\exists x_{1},\dots,x_{5},\land_{i,j\in[1,5];i\neq j}x_{i}\neq x_{j}
as an axiom, for forcing
\mathbin{\dot{\sim}}_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}
.
Thanks to this encoding, each of them can also be tested by using ATP/SMT systems.
Nevertheless, in the following, as an example, we give explicit proof for
Equation

13
.
By using the standard translation,

Equation

13
is translated into the following formula in first-order logic, where
x_{0},y_{0}
are free variables:

\displaystyle(\exists y_{1},(\exists y_{2},a(x_{0},y_{2})\land y_{2}\neq y_{1})\land y_{1}\neq y_{0})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle(\exists y_{1},(\exists y_{2},(\exists y_{3},a(x_{0},y_{3})\land y_{3}\neq y_{2})\land y_{2}\neq y_{1})\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}).

This formula is valid under
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
, which can be shown by using the axiom above (notice that under
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
,
y_{1}
on the left and
y_{1},y_{2}
on the right always exist, by taking a vertex not assigned by any variable occurring in each formula;
thus, both formulas are equivalent to the formula
\exists y,a(x_{0},y)
).
Even without the encoding to first-order logic, this equation can also be shown as follows:

\displaystyle(\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}})[a]=(a\cdot\mathsf{D})\cdot\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle=a\cdot(\mathsf{D}\cdot\mathsf{D})

(associativity law)

\displaystyle=a\cdot\top

(
\top\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 3}}}\mathsf{D}\cdot\mathsf{D}
)

\displaystyle=a\cdot(\mathsf{D}\cdot\mathsf{D}\cdot\mathsf{D})

(
\top\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 3}}}\top\cdot\mathsf{D}
and
\top\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 3}}}\mathsf{D}\cdot\mathsf{D}
)

\displaystyle=((a\cdot\mathsf{D})\cdot\mathsf{D})\cdot\mathsf{D}=(\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}})[a].

(associativity law)

See
Appendix

C
for all the equations.
∎

Lemma 37
.

The language
\bigcup_{i\in[1,21]}\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}v_{i}\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}
over
\dot{\Sigma}_{0}
is cofinite.

Proof Sketch.

It suffices to prove that for some
n\in{\rm Nature}
, the following hold:
there is no word
w\in\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}\setminus(\bigcup_{i\in[1,21]}\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}v_{i}\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*})
such that
\|w\|\geq n
(since the set
\{w\in\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}\mid\|w\|\leq n-1\}
is finite).
This holds when
n\geq 29
, which can be tested by using Z3 (an ATP/SMT system)
[
10
]

and can be checked by drawing its DFA (see
Appendix

D
, for more details).
∎

Thus, we have obtained the following:

Lemma 38
.

\dot{\Sigma}^{*}/{\mathbin{\dot{\sim}}_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}}
is finite.

Proof.

By
Lemmas

36
and
37
, we can apply
Lemma

15
,
where
<
is the shortlex order on
\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}
induced by:
\cap_{\mathsf{I}}<\cap_{\mathsf{D}}<\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}<\smile
.
By the form,
w_{i}<v_{i}
is clear for each
i\in[1,21]
.
∎

Finally,
Theorem

24
is obtained as follows:

Proof of
Theorem

24
.

We have:

\dot{\Sigma}_{0}^{*}/{\mathbin{\dot{\sim}}_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}}
is finite (
Lemma

38
)

\Longrightarrow

\dot{\Sigma}^{*}/{\mathbin{\dot{\sim}}_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}}
is finite (
Lemma

35
)

\Longrightarrow

\mathbf{T}^{\{\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}}
is finite (
Lemma

33
)

\Longrightarrow

(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}}
is finite (
Lemma

32
)

\Longrightarrow

(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}}
is finite (
Lemma

31
)

\Longrightarrow

(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq k)}/{\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}}
is finite (
Lemma

28
).
∎

Remark 39
.

The finite axiomatizability of the equational theory of
(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq k)}
over
\mathsf{REL}
immediately follows from the finiteness of
(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq k)}/{\mathbin{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}}
.

5
Conclusion

We have introduced the
k
-variable-occurrence fragment

and presented an approach for showing the decidability of the equational theory from the finiteness.
As a case study, we have proved that the equational theory of
(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq k)}
is decidable,
whereas that of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n}
is undecidable in general.
We leave the decidability open for the equational theory of CoR with
full dot-dagger alternation
(i.e.,
\mathbf{T}^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq k)}
, in this paper).
Our approach may apply to some other algebras/logics.
It would be interesting to consider the finite variable-occurrence fragment for other systems (e.g., CoR with antidomain
[
14
,
11
]
, dynamic logics
[
13
]
).
It would also be interesting to extend our result to
first-order logic with equality
(cf.
Proposition

18
)—for example, is the
k
-atomic-predicate-occurrence fragment
of the
m
variable fragment of first-order logic with equality decidable?

Appendix A
Proof of
Proposition

23

(In this section, we refer, e.g.,
[
8
]
, for standard terminologies in first-order logic.)
First, we show
Proposition

23
when
\Sigma
is a countably infinite set:

Proposition 40

(
Proposition

23
for countably infinite set
\Sigma
)
.

When
\Sigma
is a countably infinite set,
the equational theory of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n}
(resp.
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n}
) is decidable if
n\leq 1
and is undecidable if
n\geq 2
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n}
.
Case
n\leq 1
:
Let
t,s\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
.
Let
z_{1},z_{2}
be distinct variables of first-order formulas.
By using the recursive translation of
Proposition

22

[
24
]
,
let
\varphi
(resp.
\psi
) be the formula in the level
\Sigma_{1}
of the three variable fragment of first-order logic with equality with two free variables
z_{1},z_{2}

such that (w.r.t. binary relations)
\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.\varphi
(resp.
\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.\psi
)
10
10

10

We use
\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.\varphi
to denote the term having the semantics
\llbracket\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.\varphi\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}\mid\mbox{$\varphi$ holds on $\mathfrak{A}$ with all valuations mapping $z_{1}$ into $x_{1}$ and $z_{2}$ into $x_{2}$}\}
on a structure
\mathfrak{A}
. (In
[
24
]
, the notation “
[\varphi]_{z_{1}z_{2}}
” is used instead of
\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.\varphi
.)
is semantically equivalent to
t
(resp.
s
) over
\mathsf{REL}
.
Then,
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s

\iff
the formula
\forall z_{1}z_{2},\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi
is valid over
\mathsf{REL}
.
By taking the prenex normal form
11
11

11

\mathsf{REL}
does not contain the empty structure; thus we can take the prenex normal form.
,
let
\varphi\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\exists x_{1}\dots x_{n},\varphi_{0}
(resp.
\psi\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\exists y_{1}\dots y_{m},\psi_{0}
), where
\varphi_{0}
and
\psi_{0}
are quantifier-free formulas.
Then,

\displaystyle t\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s

\displaystyle\iff\lnot(\forall z_{1}z_{2},\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi)\mbox{ is satisfiable}

\displaystyle\iff(\exists z_{1}z_{2},\varphi\land\lnot\psi)\lor(\exists z_{1}z_{2},\lnot\varphi\land\psi)\mbox{ is satisfiable}

\displaystyle\iff(\exists z_{1}z_{2},(\exists x_{1}\dots x_{n},\varphi_{0})\land(\forall y_{1}\dots y_{m},\lnot\psi_{0}))

\displaystyle\qquad\lor(\exists z_{1}z_{2},(\exists y_{1}\dots y_{m},\lnot\varphi_{0})\land(\forall x_{1}\dots x_{n},\psi_{0}))\mbox{ is satisfiable}.

By taking the prenex normal form, the last sentence is equivalent to a sentence in the BSR class
[\exists^{*}\forall^{*},\mathrm{all}]_{=}
.
Because the satisfiability problem of the BSR class is decidable
[
4
,
29
]
,
the equational theory of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
over
\mathsf{REL}
is decidable.

Case
n\geq 2
:
The class
[\forall\exists\land\forall^{3},(\omega,1)]
(hence,
[\forall\exists\land\forall^{3},(0,\omega)]
) is a conservative reduction class
[
8
, Cor. 3.1.19]
; thus, the satisfiability problem of the class
[\forall\exists\land\forall^{3},(0,\omega)]
is undecidable.
Let
(\forall x,\exists y,\varphi)\land(\forall xyz,\psi)
be a sentence in the class
[\forall\exists\land\forall^{3},(0,\omega)]
, where
\varphi,\psi
are quantifier-free.
Since
(\forall xyz,\psi)
and
(\exists x,\forall y,\lnot\varphi)
are in the level
\Sigma_{2}
of the three variable fragment of first-order logic,
by using the recursive translation of
Proposition

22

[
24
]
,
let
t
(resp.
s
) be the term in
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}

such that
\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.(\forall xyz,\psi)
(resp.
\lambda z_{1}z_{2}.\exists x,\forall y,\lnot\varphi
) is semantically equivalent to
t
(resp.
s
) over
\mathsf{REL}
, where
z_{1},z_{2}
are any pairwise distinct variables.
Then, we have:

\displaystyle(\forall x,\exists y,\varphi)\land(\forall xyz,\psi)\mbox{ is satisfiable}\iff\lnot((\forall x,\exists y,\varphi)\land(\forall xyz,\psi))\mbox{ is valid}

\displaystyle\iff(\forall xyz,\psi)\to(\exists x,\forall y,\lnot\varphi)\mbox{ is valid}

\displaystyle\iff((\forall xyz,\psi)\lor(\exists x,\forall y,\lnot\varphi))\leftrightarrow(\exists x,\forall y,\lnot\varphi)\mbox{ is valid}

\displaystyle\iff t\cup s\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s.

Thus, as we can give a reduction from the satisfiability problem of the class
[\forall\exists\land\forall^{3},(0,\omega)]
,
the equational theory of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
over
\mathsf{REL}
is undecidable.
∎

A.1
Proof of
Proposition

23
when
\Sigma
is finite

Lemma 41
.

Assume that
\Sigma
is a countably infinite set.
Then, the equational theory over
\mathsf{REL}
is undecidable
for
complement-free
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
(i.e., the class of terms in
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
not having the complement operator (
-
)).

Proof.

We give a reduction from that equational theory of
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
(which is undecidable by
Proposition

23
).
By taking the complement normal form (cf.
Lemma

47
),
it suffices to give a reduction from that equational theory of terms in
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
such that the complement operator (
-
) only applies to variables.
Let
t,s
be such terms.
Let
\Sigma_{\{t,s\}}
be the finite set of all variables occurring in
t
or
s
.
Let
\bar{\bullet}\colon\Sigma_{\{t,s\}}\to(\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\{t,s\}})
be an injective map.
Let
t^{\prime},s^{\prime},{u},{u}^{\prime}
be the terms defined as follows:

\displaystyle t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mbox{ the term $t$ in which each occurrence $a^{-}$ has been replaced with the variable $\bar{a}$}

\displaystyle s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mbox{ the term $s$ in which each occurrence $a^{-}$ has been replaced with the variable $\bar{a}$}

\displaystyle{u}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bot\dagger(\bigcap_{a\in\Sigma_{\{t,s\}}}(a\cup\bar{a}))\dagger\bot

\displaystyle{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\top\cdot(\bigcup_{a\in\Sigma_{\{t,s\}}}(a\cap\bar{a}))\cdot\top.

(
{u}
and
{u}^{\prime}
are used to force
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\cup\llbracket\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}
and
\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\cap\llbracket\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\emptyset
, respectively.)
Then, we have:

\displaystyle t\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s

\displaystyle\iff(t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}(s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}.

\Longrightarrow
:
Let
\mathfrak{A}
be s.t.
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\neq\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
be the structure
\mathfrak{A}
in which
\bar{a}^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}|\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}|^{2}\setminus a^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
.
Then,
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
and
\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket s^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
hold by
\llbracket a^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
.
Also by
\bar{a}^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}=|\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}|^{2}\setminus a^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
,
\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}=|\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}|^{2}
and
\llbracket{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}=\emptyset
hold.
Thus,
\llbracket(t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}=\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\neq\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket s^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}=\llbracket(s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
.

\Longleftarrow
:
Let
\mathfrak{A}
be s.t.
\llbracket(t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\neq\llbracket(s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Then, we have
\llbracket a\cap\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\emptyset
for
a\in\Sigma_{\{t,s\}}

(
\because
If we assume
\llbracket a\cap\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\neq\emptyset
,
then
\llbracket{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}
, and thus
\llbracket(t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}=\llbracket(s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
;
so reaching a contradiction); thus
\llbracket{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\emptyset
.
Similarly, we have
\llbracket a\cup\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}
for
a\in\Sigma_{\{t,s\}}

(
\because
If we assume
\llbracket a\cup\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\neq|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}
;
then
\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\emptyset
, and thus
\llbracket(t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\emptyset=\llbracket(s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
;
so reaching a contradiction); thus
\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}
.
By
\llbracket a\cap\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\emptyset
and
\llbracket a\cup\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=|\mathfrak{A}|^{2}
, we have
\llbracket a^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket\bar{a}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
,
and thus
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
and
\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket s^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Thus,
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket t^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket(t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\neq\llbracket(s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket s^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
.

Finally, because
t^{\prime},s^{\prime}\in\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
,
{u}\in\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
,
{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
,
we have
((t^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime}),((s^{\prime}\cap{u})\cup{u}^{\prime})\in\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
.
Hence, this completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 42
.

If
\Sigma
is a non-empty set,
the equational theory of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
is undecidable.

Proof.

Let
\Sigma^{\prime}=\{a_{1},a_{2},\dots\}
be a countably infinite set.
We give a reduction from the equational theory of complement-free
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
over
\Sigma^{\prime}
(cf.
Lemma

41
).
Let
a^{\Delta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(a^{\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 1\}}\cap a^{\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 2\}})
;
note that
\llbracket a^{\Delta}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}=\{\langle x,y\rangle\mid\langle x,x\rangle,\langle y,y\rangle\in\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\}
for any
\mathfrak{A}
.
Let
\mathcal{T}(t)
be the term defined by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(a_{i})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a^{\Delta}\cap(a\cdot((a^{-}\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot a)^{i})\mbox{\quad for $i\geq 1$}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(s^{\pi})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{T}(s)^{\pi}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\top)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a^{\Delta}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(s\cup{u})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathcal{T}(s)\cup\mathcal{T}({u})

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\bot)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bot

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(s\cap{u})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathcal{T}(s)\cap\mathcal{T}({u})

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\mathsf{I})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a^{\Delta}\cap\mathsf{I}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(s\cdot{u})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathcal{T}(s)\cdot\mathcal{T}({u})

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\mathsf{D})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a^{\Delta}\cap\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(s\dagger{u})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}a^{\Delta}\cap(((a^{\Delta})^{-}\cup\mathcal{T}(s))\dagger((a^{\Delta})^{-}\cup\mathcal{T}({u}))).

(Cf.
[
22
, Lem. 11]
.
This construction can be viewed as a variant of the
relativization
in logic—the translations
(\exists x,\varphi)\leadsto(\exists x,A(x)\land\varphi)
and

(\forall x,\varphi)\leadsto(\forall x,A(x)\to\varphi
).
The construction above is refined not to increase the dot-dagger alternation.)

Claim 43
.

For every
t
such that the complement operator does not occur in
t
,

•

if
t\in\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0}
, then
\mathcal{T}(t)\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
;

•

if
t\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
, then
\mathcal{T}(t)\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
;

•

if
t\in\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
, then
\mathcal{T}(t)\in\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
.

{claimproof}

By straightforward induction on
t
using
\mathcal{T}(a_{i})\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1}
.
Note that
a^{\Delta}\in\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0}(=\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0})
.

For a structure
\mathfrak{A}
, let
\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}
be the structure defined by:

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{x\mid\langle x,x\rangle\in a^{\mathfrak{A}}\}

\displaystyle a_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\llbracket\mathcal{T}(a_{i})\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\mbox{\quad($a_{i}\in\Sigma^{\prime}$)}.

Then, the following two hold:

Claim 44
.

For every
\mathfrak{A}
and
t
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(t)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
.

{claimproof}

By induction on
t
.
Case
t=\top
:

\displaystyle\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}=|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\llbracket a^{\Delta}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

(By Def. of
\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}
)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(\top)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

Case
t=\bot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}
:
As with the case of
t=\top
.
Case
t=s\cdot{u}
:

\displaystyle\llbracket s\cdot{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}=\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}\cdot\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\cdot\llbracket\mathcal{T}({u})\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

(IH)

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s)\cdot\mathcal{T}({u})\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s\cdot{u})\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

Case
t=s\cup{u},s\cap{u}
:
As with the case of
t=s\cdot{u}
.
Case
t=s\dagger{u}
(note that the operator
\dagger
depends on the universe):
Note that if
\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\llbracket s^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}=|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|^{2}\setminus\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|^{2}\setminus\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

(By assumption)

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|^{2}\cap\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s)^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket a^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{T}(s)^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

By using this, we have:

\displaystyle\llbracket s\dagger{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|^{2}\setminus(\llbracket s^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}}\cdot\llbracket{u}^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}})

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{t}}|^{2}\setminus(\llbracket a^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{T}(s)^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\cdot\llbracket a^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{T}({u})^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}})

(By IH with the above)

\displaystyle=\llbracket a^{\Delta}\cap((a^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{T}(s)^{-})\cdot(a^{\Delta}\cap\mathcal{T}({u})^{-}))^{-}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket a^{\Delta}\cap(((a^{\Delta})^{-}\cup\mathcal{T}(s))\dagger((a^{\Delta})^{-}\cup\mathcal{T}({u})))\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s\dagger{u})\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

Hence, this completes the proof.

Claim 45

(cf.
[
22
, Lem. 11]
)
.

For every
\mathfrak{A}
, there is a structure
\mathfrak{B}
such that
\mathfrak{A}=\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}
.

{claimproof}

Let
\mathfrak{B}=\langle|\mathfrak{B}|,\{a^{\mathfrak{B}}\}_{a\in\{a\}}\rangle
be the structure defined by:

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{B}|

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}|\mathfrak{A}|\cup\{\langle x,y,i,j\rangle\mid\langle x,y\rangle\in\llbracket a_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}},j\in[1,i]\};

\displaystyle a^{\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathsf{I}_{|\mathfrak{A}|}\cup\{\langle x,\langle x,y,i,1\rangle\rangle,\langle\langle x,y,i,i\rangle,y\rangle\mid\langle x,y\rangle\in\llbracket a_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\{\langle\langle x,y,i,j-1\rangle,\langle x,y,i,j\rangle\rangle\mid\langle x,y\rangle\in\llbracket a_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}},j\in[2,i]\}.

Here, we assume that
|\mathfrak{A}|
and
\{\langle x,y,i,j\rangle\mid\langle x,y\rangle\in\llbracket a_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}},j\in[1,i]\}
are disjoint (the other case can be shown in the same manner by renaming vertices).
The following is an illustration of an example of the conversion from
\mathfrak{A}
to
\mathfrak{B}
:

a_{1}
a_{1}
a_{2}

\leadsto

a
a
a
a
a
a
a
a
a
a

By the construction of
\mathfrak{B}
, we have:

\displaystyle\{x\mid\langle x,x\rangle\in a^{\mathfrak{B}}\}

\displaystyle=|\mathfrak{A}|

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathcal{T}(a_{i})\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle=a_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}.

Thus we have
|\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}|=|\mathfrak{A}|
and
a_{i}^{\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}}=a_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}
.
Hence,
\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}=\mathfrak{A}
.

From these two, we have:

\displaystyle t\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s

\displaystyle\iff\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\mbox{ for some $\mathfrak{A}$}

\displaystyle\iff\llbracket t\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}}\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}}\mbox{ for some $\mathfrak{B}$}

(
\Longrightarrow
:
45
.
\Longleftarrow
: By letting
\mathfrak{A}=\mathfrak{B}^{\mathrm{t}}
)

\displaystyle\iff\llbracket\mathcal{T}(t)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{B}}\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\llbracket\mathcal{T}(s)\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{B}}\mbox{ for some $\mathfrak{B}$}

(
44
)

\displaystyle\iff\mathcal{T}(t)\not\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathcal{T}(s).

Because
t,s
are in
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
, we have that
\mathcal{T}(t),\mathcal{T}(s)
are in
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{2}
(
43
).
Hence, since
\mathcal{T}(t),\mathcal{T}(s)
contains the one variable
a
, this completes the proof.
∎

Therefore,
Proposition

23
holds even if
\Sigma
is an non-empty finite set (particularly if
\mathop{\#}\Sigma=1
):

Proposition 46

(restatement of
Proposition

23
)
.

Assume that
\Sigma
is a non-empty finite set.
The equational theory of
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n}
(resp.
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{n}
) is decidable if
n\leq 1

and is undecidable if
n\geq 2
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

42
.
∎

Appendix B
Proof completion for
Lemma

32

B.1
Complement normal form

Lemma 47

(equations for the complement normal form)
.

The following holds:

\displaystyle\top^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\bot

\displaystyle\bot^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\top

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{I}

\displaystyle(s\cup{u})^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{-}\cap{u}^{-}

\displaystyle(s\cap{u})^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{-}\cup{u}^{-}

\displaystyle(s^{-})^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s

\displaystyle(s^{\pi})^{-}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}(s^{-})^{\pi}.

Proof.

Easy.
∎

Remark 48
.

Whereas
(t\cdot s)^{-}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t^{-}\dagger s^{-}
and
(t\dagger s)^{-}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t^{-}\cdot s^{-}
,
we do not need them in the following because the complement operator does not apply to either
\cdot
or
\dagger
by the definition of
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{k}
and
\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{k}
.

Lemma 49
.

Let
t\in(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
and
a\in\Sigma
.
There are
t_{0}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}\cup\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
and
t_{1}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{-\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{0}[t_{1}/a]
.

Proof.

For
t\in(\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
:
By applying the rewriting rules induced from the equations in
Lemma

47
(from left to right) as much as possible,
we can obtain a term
t^{\prime}\in(\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that

t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t^{\prime}
and the complement operators (
-
) only apply to a variable.
(Note that
\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0}=\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,-,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}\cup\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}
.)
Then, there are
t_{0}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}\cup\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
and
t_{1}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{-\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that
t^{\prime}=t_{0}[t_{1}/a]
.
Since
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{0}[t_{1}/a]
, we have obtained such
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
.
For
t\in(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
:
By easy induction on
t
using the case for
t\in(\Pi^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{0})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
.
∎

B.2
Projection normal form (PNF)

Lemma 50

(equations for the projection normal form)
.

The following holds:

\displaystyle\top^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\top

\displaystyle\bot^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\bot

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\begin{cases}\mathsf{I}&(\pi(1)\neq\pi(2))\\
\top&(\pi(1)=\pi(2))\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\begin{cases}\mathsf{D}&(\pi(1)\neq\pi(2))\\
\bot&(\pi(1)=\pi(2))\end{cases}

\displaystyle(s\cup{u})^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{\pi}\cup{u}^{\pi}

\displaystyle(s\cap{u})^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{\pi}\cap{u}^{\pi}

\displaystyle(s^{-})^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}(s^{\pi})^{-}

\displaystyle(s^{\pi})^{\pi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{\pi\circ\pi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle(s\cdot{u})^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\begin{cases}s\cdot{u}&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 2\})\\
{u}^{\smile}\cdot s^{\smile}&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 1\})\\
(s\cap{u}^{\smile})\cdot\top&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 1\})\\
\top\cdot(s^{\smile}\cap{u})&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 2\})\end{cases}

\displaystyle(s\dagger{u})^{\pi}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\begin{cases}s\dagger{u}&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 2\})\\
{u}^{\smile}\dagger s^{\smile}&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 1\})\\
(s\cup{u}^{\smile})\dagger\bot&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 1\})\\
\bot\dagger(s^{\smile}\cup{u})&(\pi=\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 2\})\end{cases}.

Proof.

They are also easy, from the semantics.
For example,
for
(s\cup{u})^{\pi}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{\pi}\cup{u}^{\pi}
,

\displaystyle\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket(s\cup{u})^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{\pi(1)},x_{\pi(2)}\rangle\in\llbracket s\cup{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{\pi(1)},x_{\pi(2)}\rangle\in\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\lor\langle x_{\pi(1)},x_{\pi(2)}\rangle\in\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket s^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\lor\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket{u}^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket s^{\pi}\cup{u}^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

For
(s^{\pi})^{\pi^{\prime}}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}s^{\pi\circ\pi^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket(s^{\pi})^{\pi^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{\pi^{\prime}(1)},x_{\pi^{\prime}(2)}\rangle\in\llbracket s^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{\pi(\pi^{\prime}(1))},x_{\pi(\pi^{\prime}(2))}\rangle\in\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{(\pi\circ\pi^{\prime})(1)},x_{(\pi\circ\pi^{\prime})(2)}\rangle\in\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket s^{\pi\circ\pi^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

For
(s\cdot{u})^{\pi}
, we distinguish the following cases:
Case
\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 2\}
: Clear.
Case
\pi=\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 1\}
:

\displaystyle\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket(s\cdot{u})^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{2},x_{1}\rangle\in\llbracket s\cdot{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\exists z,\langle x_{2},z\rangle\in\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\land\langle z,x_{1}\rangle\in\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\exists z,\langle z,x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket s^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\land\langle x_{1},z\rangle\in\llbracket{u}^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket{u}^{\pi}\cdot s^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

Case
\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 1\}
:

\displaystyle\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket(s\cdot{u})^{\pi}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{1},x_{1}\rangle\in\llbracket s\cdot{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\exists z,\langle x_{1},z\rangle\in\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\land\langle z,x_{1}\rangle\in\llbracket{u}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\exists z,\langle x_{1},z\rangle\in\llbracket s\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\land\langle x_{1},z\rangle\in\llbracket{u}^{\smile}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\exists z,\langle x_{1},z\rangle\in\llbracket s\cap{u}^{\smile}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}\land\langle z,x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\iff\langle x_{1},x_{2}\rangle\in\llbracket(s\cap{u}^{\smile})\cdot\top\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{A}}.

Case
\pi=\{1\mapsto 2,2\mapsto 2\}
:
As with the case of
\pi=\{1\mapsto 1,2\mapsto 1\}
.
∎

Lemma 51

(decomposition by projection normal form)
.

Let
t\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}\cup\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
and
a\in\Sigma
.
Then, there are
t_{0}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
and
t_{1}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{0}[t_{1}/a]
.

Proof.

By applying the rewriting rules induced from the equations in
Lemma

50
(from left to right) as much as possible,
we can obtain a term
t^{\prime}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}\cup\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that

t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t^{\prime}
and the projection operators
\pi
only apply to a variable.
Then,
for the term
t^{\prime}
, there are
t_{0}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
and
t_{1}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that
t^{\prime}=t_{0}[t_{1}/a]
.
Since
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{0}[t_{1}/a]
, we have obtained such
t_{0}
and
t_{1}
.
∎

B.3
Union normal form

Lemma 52

(eliminating
\bot
and
\top
)
.

For every
t\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
,
there is
t_{0}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{0}
.

Proof.

By
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D}
and
\top\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{I}\cup\mathsf{D}
.
let
t_{0}
be the term
t
in which
\bot
has been replaced with
\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D}
and
\top
has been replaced with
\mathsf{I}\cup\mathsf{D}
.
Then,
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{0}
and
t_{0}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cdot,\cap,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
.
∎

Lemma 53

(equations for the union normal form)
.

The following holds:

\displaystyle(s_{1}\cup s_{2})\cap{u}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}(s_{1}\cap{u})\cup(s_{2}\cap{u})

\displaystyle{u}\cap(s_{1}\cup s_{2})

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}({u}\cap s_{1})\cup({u}\cap s_{2})

\displaystyle(s_{1}\cup s_{2})\cdot{u}

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}(s_{1}\cdot{u})\cup(s_{2}\cdot{u})

\displaystyle{u}\cdot(s_{1}\cup s_{2})

\displaystyle\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}({u}\cdot s_{1})\cup({u}\cdot s_{2}).

Proof.

Easy.
∎

Lemma 54

(decomposition by union normal form)
.

For every
t\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
,
there are
n\geq 1
and
t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\in\mathbf{T}^{\{\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}
such that
t\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t_{1}\cup\dots\cup t_{n}
.

Proof.

By straightforward induction on
t
using the equations in
Lemma

53
.
∎

B.4
Proof of
Lemma

32

Lemma 55

(restatement of
Lemma

32
)
.

If
\mathbf{T}^{\{\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite,

(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite.

Proof.

We have:

\mathbf{T}^{\{\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite

(
\because
the assumption)

\Longrightarrow

\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite

(
\because

Lemma

54
)

\Longrightarrow

\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite

(
\because

Lemma

52
)

\Longrightarrow

\mathbf{T}^{\{\cup,\cap,\cdot,\bot,\top,\mathsf{I},\mathsf{D}\}\cup\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite

(
Lemma

51
with the finiteness of
\mathbf{T}^{\{\pi\mid\pi\in[1,2]^{[1,2]}\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
)

\Longrightarrow

(\Sigma^{\mathrm{CoR}}_{1})_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
is finite.

(
Lemma

49
with the finiteness of
\mathbf{T}^{\{-\}}_{(\mathop{\mathrm{vo}}\leq 1)}/{\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}}
)

∎

Appendix C
Proof Completion of
Lemma

36

Remark 56
.

Using the standard encoding to formulas of first-order logic
[
30
]
, we can automatically check the validity of these equations.
See
[
25
]
for the TPTP files—they are at least checked by Z3 (Z3tptp 4.8.11.0)
[
10
]
, Vampire 4.7 (linked with Z3 4.8.13.0)
[
17
]
, and CVC5 1.0.3
[
3
]
.
Our encoding is based on the encoding into first-order formulas
[
30
]
(see also
[
23
,
24
]
).
Note that there is another earlier presented TPTP-encoding for the calculus of relations, by Höfner and Struth
[
15
]
, which is based on axioms of relation algebras.

Apart from the automated checking above, in the following, we present an explicit proof for each equation (w.r.t.
\sim_{\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}}
).

For
\cap_{\mathsf{I}}=\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}
:

\displaystyle a\cap\mathsf{I}

\displaystyle=a\cap(\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{I})

(
\mathsf{I}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{I}
)

\displaystyle=(a\cap\mathsf{I})\cap\mathsf{I}.

(associativity law)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{D}}=\cap_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{D}}
:

\displaystyle a\cap\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle=a\cap(\mathsf{D}\cap\mathsf{D})

(
\mathsf{D}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{D}\cap\mathsf{D}
)

\displaystyle=(a\cap\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{D}.

(associativity law)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{I}}=\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\smile
:

\displaystyle a\cap\mathsf{I}

\displaystyle=a^{\smile}\cap\mathsf{I}.

(
t\cap\mathsf{I}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t^{\smile}\cap\mathsf{I}
)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{I}}=\smile\cap_{\mathsf{I}}
:

\displaystyle a\cap\mathsf{I}

\displaystyle=a^{\smile}\cap\mathsf{I}

(
t\cap\mathsf{I}\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t^{\smile}\cap\mathsf{I}
)

\displaystyle=(a\cap\mathsf{I})^{\smile}.

(PNF;
Lemma

50
)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cap_{\mathsf{D}}=\cap_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}
:

\displaystyle(a\cap\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I}

\displaystyle=a\cap(\mathsf{D}\cap\mathsf{I})

(associativity law)

\displaystyle=a\cap(\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D})

(commutativity law)

\displaystyle=(a\cap\mathsf{I})\cap\mathsf{D}.

(associativity law)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{D}}\smile=\smile\cap_{\mathsf{D}}
:

\displaystyle a^{\smile}\cap\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle=(a\cap\mathsf{D})^{\smile}.

(PNF)

For
\varepsilon=\smile\smile
:

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=a^{\smile\smile}.

(PNF)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}=\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cap_{\mathsf{D}}
:

\displaystyle(a\cap\mathsf{I})\cap\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle=a\cap(\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D})

(associativity law)

\displaystyle=a\cap\bot

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D}
)

\displaystyle=\bot

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t\cap\bot
)

\displaystyle=\bot\cdot\mathsf{D}

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\bot\cdot t
)

\displaystyle=(a\cap\bot)\cdot\mathsf{D}

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t\cap\bot
)

\displaystyle=(a\cap(\mathsf{D}\cap\mathsf{I}))\cdot\mathsf{D}

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{D}\cap\mathsf{I}
)

\displaystyle=((a\cap\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D}.

(associativity law)

For
\cap_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}=\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}
:

\displaystyle(a\cap\mathsf{I})\cap\mathsf{D}

\displaystyle=a\cap(\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D})

(associativity law)

\displaystyle=a\cap\bot

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}\mathsf{I}\cap\mathsf{D}
)

\displaystyle=\bot

(
\bot\sim_{\mathsf{REL}}t\cap\bot
)

\displaystyle=((a\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I}.

(

\displaystyle\quad\land x_{1}\neq y_{2})\land y_{2}\neq y_{1})\land x_{1}\neq y_{1}))\land x_{0}\neq y_{1})\land y_{1}\neq y_{0})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(\exists x_{1},(\exists y_{1},((\exists x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land((\exists y_{2},(

\displaystyle\quad(\exists x_{3},x_{2}\neq x_{3}\land(a(x_{3},y_{2})\land x_{3}\neq y_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad\land x_{2}\neq y_{2})\land y_{2}\neq y_{1})\land x_{2}\neq y_{1}))\land x_{1}\neq y_{1})\land y_{1}\neq y_{0})\land x_{1}\neq y_{0}).

By taking the prenex normal form on each side, this formula is equivalent to the following:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ y_{1}\ y_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land y_{3}\neq y_{2}\land y_{2}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq y_{1}\land x_{1}\neq y_{1}\\
&\quad\land x_{1}\neq y_{2}\land x_{2}\neq y_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ x_{3}\ y_{1}\ y_{2},a(x_{3},y_{2})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{2}\neq x_{3}\\
&\quad\land x_{3}\neq y_{2}\land y_{2}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\\
&\quad\land x_{1}\neq y_{0}\land x_{1}\neq y_{1}\\
&\quad\land x_{2}\neq y_{1}\land x_{2}\neq y_{2}\end{aligned}\right).

For the left-hand side formula, over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ y_{1}\ y_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land y_{3}\neq y_{2}\land y_{2}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq y_{1}\land x_{1}\neq y_{1}\\
&\quad\land x_{1}\neq y_{2}\land x_{2}\neq y_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists\ x_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\land x_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land\exists y_{2},y_{2}\neq y_{3}\land y_{2}\neq x_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\land\exists x_{1},x_{1}\neq x_{0}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{1}\neq y_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\land\exists y_{1},y_{1}\neq y_{2}\\
&\hskip 50.00008pt\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\land y_{1}\neq x_{0}\land y_{1}\neq x_{1}\\
\end{aligned}\right)

(prenex)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists\ x_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\land x_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land\exists y_{2},y_{2}\neq y_{3}\land y_{2}\neq x_{2}\\
&\quad\quad\land\exists x_{1},x_{1}\neq x_{0}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{1}\neq y_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

(
\mathsf{True}\leftrightarrow\exists y_{1},y_{1}\neq y_{2}\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\land y_{1}\neq x_{0}\land y_{1}\neq x_{1}
over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists\ x_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\land x_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land\exists y_{2},y_{2}\neq y_{3}\land y_{2}\neq x_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

(
\mathsf{True}\leftrightarrow\exists x_{1},x_{1}\neq x_{0}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{1}\neq y_{2}
over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 4}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists\ x_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\land x_{2}\neq y_{3})

(
\mathsf{True}\leftrightarrow\exists y_{2},y_{2}\neq y_{3}\land y_{2}\neq x_{2}
over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 3}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists\ x\ y,a(x,y)\land x\neq y).

By the same argument, for the right-hand side formula, over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ x_{3}\ y_{1}\ y_{2},a(x_{3},y_{2})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{2}\neq x_{3}\\
&\quad\land x_{3}\neq y_{2}\land y_{2}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\\
&\quad\land x_{1}\neq y_{0}\land x_{1}\neq y_{1}\\
&\quad\land x_{2}\neq y_{1}\land x_{2}\neq y_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists\ x\ y,a(x,y)\land x\neq y).

Combining them, the translated formula from
Equation

⋆

9
is valid over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
.

For
\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\smile\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\smile\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}=\smile\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\smile\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\cap_{\mathsf{I}}\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}\smile\cdot_{\mathsf{D}}
:

\displaystyle(((((((a\cdot\mathsf{D})^{\smile}\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D})^{\smile}\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D})

\displaystyle=((((\mathsf{D}\cdot((\mathsf{D}\cdot(a\cdot\mathsf{D}))\cap\mathsf{I}))\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D})

(PNF)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{D}\cdot((\mathsf{D}\cdot((((\mathsf{D}\cdot a^{\smile})\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D}))\cap\mathsf{I}))

(
\star
11)

\displaystyle=(((((((a\cdot\mathsf{D})^{\smile}\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D})^{\smile}\cdot\mathsf{D})\cap\mathsf{I})\cdot\mathsf{D})^{\smile}.

(PNF)

Here, for
Equation

⋆

11
, we consider the translated first-order formula:

\displaystyle(\exists y_{1},((\exists y_{2},(\exists x_{1},x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land((\exists x_{2},

\displaystyle x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land(\exists y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\land y_{3}\neq y_{2}))\land x_{1}=y_{2}))\land y_{2}\neq y_{1})\land x_{0}=y_{1})\land y_{1}\neq y_{0})

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(\exists x_{1},x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land((\exists x_{2},x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land(\exists y_{1},

\displaystyle\quad((\exists y_{2},(\exists x_{3},x_{2}\neq x_{3}\land a(y_{2},x_{3}))\land y_{2}\neq y_{1})\land x_{2}=y_{1})\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}))\land x_{1}=y_{0})).

By taking the prenex normal form on each side, this formula is equivalent to the following:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ y_{1}\ y_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\\
&\quad\land y_{0}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{2}\land y_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land x_{0}=y_{1}\land x_{1}=y_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ x_{3}\ y_{1}\ y_{2},a(y_{2},x_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{2}\neq x_{3}\\
&\quad\land y_{0}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{2}\\
&\quad\land x_{1}=y_{0}\land x_{2}=y_{1}\end{aligned}\right).

For the left-hand side formula, we have:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ y_{1}\ y_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\\
&\quad\land y_{0}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{2}\land y_{2}\neq y_{3}\\
&\quad\land x_{0}=y_{1}\land x_{1}=y_{2}\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\\
&\quad\land y_{0}\neq x_{0}\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq y_{3}\end{aligned}\right)

(
{\varphi}[z^{\prime}/z]\leftrightarrow\exists z,\varphi\land z=z^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists x_{2}\ y_{3},a(x_{2},y_{3})\land x_{0}\neq y_{0})

(
\mathsf{True}\leftrightarrow\exists x_{1},x_{1}\neq x_{0}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{1}\neq y_{3}
over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists x\ y,a(x,y)\land x_{0}\neq y_{0}).

(By renaming)

For the right-hand side formula, we have:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{1}\ x_{2}\ x_{3}\ y_{1}\ y_{2},a(y_{2},x_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq x_{1}\land x_{1}\neq x_{2}\land x_{2}\neq x_{3}\\
&\quad\land y_{0}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{2}\\
&\quad\land x_{2}=y_{1}\land x_{1}=y_{0}\\
\end{aligned}\right)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow\left(\begin{aligned} &\exists x_{3}\ y_{1}\ y_{2},a(y_{2},x_{3})\\
&\quad\land x_{0}\neq y_{0}\land y_{0}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq x_{3}\\
&\quad\land y_{0}\neq y_{1}\land y_{1}\neq y_{2}\\
\end{aligned}\right)

(
{\varphi}[z^{\prime}/z]\leftrightarrow\exists z,\varphi\land z=z^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists x_{3}\ y_{2},a(y_{2},x_{3})\land x_{0}\neq y_{0})

(
\mathsf{True}\leftrightarrow\exists y_{1},y_{1}\neq y_{0}\land y_{1}\neq x_{3}\land y_{1}\neq y_{0}\land y_{1}\neq y_{2}
over
\mathsf{REL}_{\geq 5}
)

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow(\exists x\ y,a(x,y)\land x_{0}\neq y_{0}).

(By renaming)

Hence, we have proved all the equations in
Figure

1
.
